| thorn | kataʻ | kator | kata | katoi. |
| ewe | kech | kech-ir | kechir | kechēr. |
| club | rungu | rungu-s | rungu | rungu-s. |
| salt-lick | ñgeñg | ñgeñg-wa | ñgeñg | ñgeñg-wa. |
| sugar-cane, stubble | mochen | mopa | mopcho | mop. |
| kid | wawa | wara | aruwa | are. |
| girl | chep-to | ti-pin | chepto (with article) | tipin. |
| bull | kiruk | kir | kiruk | kiruk. |
| animal, meat | pendoʻ *or* piny | panye, pan | pendo (with article) | pany. |
| bed | pareiy | pareiy-us | pire-yus | pireyuos. |

##### CLASS VIII
###### COLLECTIVE NOUNS.

This class includes things generally spoken of collectively. Singulars can sometimes be formed according to rules of Class I.

> ¹ From Mr. Hollis's *The Nandi Language and Folklore*.
> ² The corresponding words are considered irregular in Nandi.

*(Page 62)*

###### A
Examples:

| *English.* | *Suk.* | *English.* | *Suk.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| pus | perutit.¹ | flour | pesia. |
| bee | sakam. | mud (for dressing hair) | munyan. |
| dung | piyat. | mud | ñgaiyam. |
| fruit | akwaʻ. | saliva | ñgul. |
| honey | komat. | honey-wine | koman.³ |
| medicine | saket. | milk | cheghwa. |
| news | lugoi. | curdled milk | keleloʻ. |
| poison | mwônoʻ. | gruel | musar. |
| property | tukun.² | food | omisioʻ. |
| cloud of locusts | talamnya. | work | ighisioʻ. |
| earth, soil | nyuñgwin. | white ants | taugh. |
| corn | pagh. | ants in general | sungok. |
| Indian corn | pembe. | brown ants | pirech. |
| millet | musong. | wind | yamat. |
| eleusine grain | matai. | | |

###### B
A few words are the same both in singular and plural, as:

| *English.* | *Sing. and Plural.* | *English.* | *Sing. and Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| custom | paghisioʻ. | nipple | ketan. |
| heart | mughulo. | boundary | rotiot. |
| lap | kipis. | warrior | muren. |

##### EXAMPLES OF IRREGULAR SUBSTANTIVES.

| *English.* | *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| small boy | kara-ch-inin | kara-ch-ona.⁴ |
| bamboo | teichan | terka. |
| bone | kôwôʻ | kô. |
| fish | käoghwa | kagha. |
| goat | aran, aretan | ñgaror. |
| hand | hegh | he'-wun. |
| honey-barrel | mweghin | mweghin-ton.⁵ |
| water-gourd | set | set-on. |
| mouth | kôt | kôtoʻ. |

> ¹ This is the word for a wound, or scar, with the affix *it*.
> ² Lit. 'things', pl. of *kue*.
> ³ Cf. 'honey' = *komat*.
> ⁴ *Ch* is the *ch* in *chi*, pl. *pi-ch*; *-inin* is possibly a diminutive suffix of which *-ona* is the plural.
> ⁵ Also *mweghin-t-is*.

*(Page 63)*

| *English.* | *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| pud. mulieb. | sei | sei-yon.¹ |
| visitor | ton | tô. |
| armpit | kolkol | kulkul-wak. |
| arrow | kôtat | kôti. |
| sack | sambar | sambar-us. |
| battle | luk | luk-us. |
| cord | perkeiy-o | perkeiy-i. |
| dewlap | thaghaʻ | theghiʻ. |
| ant-hill | tul | tul-on-oi. |
| ghost | onyet | (onyet-ai), hoi. |

#### ADJECTIVES

Adjectives, except when they are employed predicatively, are used only in conjunction with the relative *nya*, *nyo*, *nyô*, plural *cha*, *cho*, *chô*. Thus:
   chi nya karam, a good man (*lit.* a man who is good);
   but karam chi-chino, this man is good.

Adjectives may be divided into two classes:

##### A
Those which form their plural by the addition of *-ech* to the singular form. Examples:

| *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ananga nya tekerel (a new cloth.) | anangis cha tekerel-ech (new clothes.) |
| munuñg nya cherut (a naked boy.) | munu'² cha cherut-ech (naked boys.) |
| sirimnyon nya nikis (a heavy chain.) | sirimtin cha nikis-ech (heavy chains.) |
| kue nya parâ (a broad article.) | tukun cha parâ-ech (broad articles.) |
| pendoʻ nya nyaril (raw meat.) | piny cha nyaril-ech (raw meats.) |
| rotwa nya ngatip (a sharp knife.) | rotoi cha ngatip-ech (sharp knives.) |

> ¹ If the sing. were the pl. and the pl. the sing. this word would be regular according to Class I.
> ² It is quite legitimate to omit the awkward *ñg* before *cha* or any consonant.

*(Page 64)*

Slightly irregular are:

| *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- |
| chi nya ghaʻ (a bad man.) | pi' cha ghach (bad men.) |
| munu' nya togh (a black boy.) | munu' cha tu-w-ech (black boys.) |

##### B
Most of the remaining adjectives make their plural by the addition of *-e*, or *-i*, or *ei*, and occasionally *ne*.

| *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- |
| or nya tantan (a narrow road.) | orten cha tantan-i (narrow roads.) |
| adasti nya taitagh (a bad egg.) | adastin cha taitagh-e (bad eggs.) |
| kue ny' anyin (a sweet thing.) | tukun ch' anyin-ei (sweet things.) |
| pendoʻ nya kaghet (cold meat.) | piny cha kaghet-ei (cold meats.) |
| sigirioʻ nya koskos (a cheap donkey.) | sigirei cha koskos-e (cheap donkeys.) |
| añgoli nya pertat (a fast horse.) | añgolitin cha pertat-i (fast horses.) |
| chi nya kasarte (a sick man.) | pi' cha kasôrte-ne (sick men.) |

A few adjectives remain the same in the plural, as:

| *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- |
| suswon nya kakur (a ripe [ready for grazing] blade of grass.) | sus cha kakur (ripe grass.) |

##### EXAMPLES OF IRREGULAR ADJECTIVES.

| *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- |
| aran nya wō (a large goat.) | ñgaror cha echen¹ (large goats.) |
| egh nya telene (a gentle bullock.) | eghin cha tolosi (gentle bullocks.) |
| musūk nya āuü (a hard pole.) | musūk cha ōghei (hard poles.) |
| chepto nya siset (a quiet girl.) | tipin cha sisot (quiet girls.) |
| kadich nya meneñg (a small boat.) | kaditin cha mingech² (small boats.) |

> ¹ Cf. Nandi *oo*, pl. *echen*. When a word in Nandi begins with a vowel it is common for the corresponding word in Suk to prefix a *w*. Cf. different, Nandi *alak* (pl.), Suk *walak*.
> ² Nandi *mining*, pl. *mingech*.

*(Page 65)*

Adjectives may be made from nouns or adverbs by the addition of the prefix *le*¹ combined with the relative form as above.

| *Base* | *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| kērsang (an idle person.) | munuñg nya le-kērsang (an idle boy.) | munu cha le-kērsong (idle boys.) |
| sekeiy (a stingy person.) | kôrkôʻ nya le-sekeiy (a stingy old woman.) | kôru cha le-sekeiy-ut (stingy old women.) |
| selwaʻ (a fool.) | chi nya le-selwaʻ (a foolish man.) | pi' cha le-selwei (foolish men.) |

Similarly:
   chi nya le-miwon, a poor man; pl. pi' cha le-mi, poor men.

| *Base* | *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| kuliʻ (adverb: in vain). | kerep nya le-kuliʻ (an empty basket.) | kerep-ai cha le-kuliʻ (empty baskets.) |

The foregoing can all be put shortly as follows, but in this case the attributes can hardly be called adjectives, e.g.:
   munuñg kērsang, a boy, an idle person.
   kôrkôʻ sekeiy, an old woman, a stingy person.
   kerep kuliʻ, a basket, a, to no purpose.
&c., &c., &c.

*Much*, *many* are represented by *cha-chang*, more rarely *cho*, *chô*:
   pi'-cha chang, many men.

*Little*, *few* by *ñgeri*:
   ñgaror cho ñgeri, a paltry flock of goats.

*How many* by *ata* (*vide* p. 75):
   pich-ata, how many men?; ñgaror-ata, how many goats?

*Different* by *anga*, plural *w-alak*:
   chi anga, a different man; pl. pi' walak.
   tuku' walak, different things.

*Male and female* have been discussed under 'gender', q.v., p. 50.

*Right-hand*, ewu-tagh; *left-hand*, ewu-let.

A rare adjectival form is made by the use of the prep. *pa*, of, with a noun:
   po' pa maʻ, water of fire; hence 'hot water'.²
   anangis cha pa-kainy, clothes of time; i.e. old clothes.

> ¹ This particle *le* may possibly = 'like', as seen in *le-nyô-nyi*, like this. If so, then the expression *munuñg-nya-le-kērsang*, the boy who is like a lazy person, seems unnecessarily clumsy.
> ² Cf. Suahili *maji ya moto*.

*(Signature at foot: 1268 F)*

*(Page 66)*

##### COMPARISON OF ADJECTIVES.

The comparative is represented in three ways. There is no inflexion marking degrees of comparison in Suk.
1. By a somewhat clumsy periphrasis, as in the following example:
   wō rotwa-nyi meneñg nyino
   lit. great is this knife, small is that.
   i.e. this knife is larger than that.

2. By the use of the word *mba*, rather than:
   wō chi-chino mba nyino
   this man is great rather than that man.

3. By the use of the word *mwañgwa*, more so.
   karam Aijigwa mwañgwa Andaret
   lit. good is Aijigwa; more so is Andaret.
   i.e. Andaret is better than Aijigwa.

##### SUPERLATIVE.

As in Nandi the superlative is generally represented by the adjective in its simple form:
   ñgô nya karam? who is best?

*Nyaman*, indeed, is also used as in the following example:
   wō Akanichôñg mwañgwa Lowelan: walô wō Karôle nyaman
   A great chief is Akanichong, greater is Lowelan, but greatest is Karôle.

*Kōchĭu*, truly, is also common.
   karam kōchiyu, very fine indeed.

A neat idiomatic way of expressing the superlative is as follows:
   ocha karam ma-mi-nye
   lit. I am fine, there is not (any one finer).

#### NUMERALS

##### CARDINALS.

| *English* | *Suk* |
| :--- | :--- |
| one | okoñgô. |
| two | oghieñg. |
| three | somok. |

*(Page 67)*

| *English* | *Suk* |
| :--- | :--- |
| four | añgwan. |
| five | mūt. |
| six | mut ngô okoñgô, akan-kapei.¹ |
| seven | mut ngô oghieñg, akan-kare.¹ |
| eight | mut ngô somok, akan-kowuni.¹ |
| nine | mut ngô añgwan, akan-ko-mwan.¹ |
| ten | mut ngô taman. |
| eleven | taman ngô okoñgô. |
| twelve | taman ngô oghieñg. |
| thirteen | taman ngô somok. |
| fourteen | taman ngô añgwan. |
| fifteen | taman ngô mut. |
| sixteen | taman ngô mut ngô okoñgô. |
| seventeen | taman ngô mut ngô oghieñg. |
| eighteen | taman ngô mut ngô somok. |
| nineteen | taman ngô mut ngô añgwan. |
| twenty | tiptem, taman ut-oghieñg. |
| twenty-one | tiptem ngô okoñgô. |
| thirty | sosom. |
| forty | artam. |
| fifty | konom. |
| sixty | konom ngô taman. |
| seventy | konom ngô tiptem *or* listam.² |
| eighty | konom ngô sosom. |
| ninety | konom ngô artam. |
| one hundred | pokol. |

Large numbers above 100, pokoloi-pokoloi.
Cardinal numbers follow the substantive:
   pelion okoñgô, one elephant.
   osis oghieñg, two days.

The Suk, like the Masai and Nandi, have a system of counting with their fingers. Such gestures nearly always accompany the spoken cardinal.

*one.* The hand is held up closed with the exception of the index finger which is outstretched. The hand must be shaken from the wrist.

> ¹ These are pure Turkana but are often employed.
> ² This word seems to refer to 40 goats and 30 sheep.

*(Signature at foot: F 2)*

*(Page 68)*

*two.* The same as for one, except that the first and second fingers are outstretched.
*three.* Second, third, and fourth fingers are outstretched, while the tip of the first is closed on the tip of the thumb, forming thereby a circle.
*four.* Third and fourth and second and first fingers outstretched close together with a division between the two groups.
*five.* Hand closed but thumb tip appearing between first and second finger.
*six.* The little finger of the right hand is inserted in the closed left fist at the thumb end.
*seven.* As for six, only third and fourth fingers are inserted.
*eight.* As for seven, but with the addition of the second finger.
*nine.* The same as eight, but with the addition of the fourth finger.
*ten.* The first finger is 'flicked' out from the thumb and remains outstretched.
*eleven.* The right hand is open with the exception of the little finger which is closed upon the palm.
*twelve.* The same as eleven with the addition of the third finger closed.
*thirteen.* The same as twelve with the addition of the second finger closed.
*fourteen.* The same as thirteen with the addition of the first finger closed.
*fifteen.* The sign for ten followed by the sign for five.
*sixteen, seventeen, eighteen, nineteen.* The sign for ten followed by the sign for five followed again by the signs for one, two, three, and four respectively.
*twenty.* The first, second, third, and fourth fingers are flapped simultaneously against the palm of the hand.
*twenty-one, &c.* The sign for twenty followed by the sign for one, &c.
*thirty.* Join thumb and second finger, outstretch the first, and close the rest.
*forty.* Outstretch the whole hand divided as for 'four' and shake horizontally.
*fifty.* The thumb is placed between the first and second finger and the hand half closed and shaken.
*one hundred.* All fingers are flicked out from both previously closed fists.

*(Page 69)*

##### ORDINALS.

Ordinals are formed from the cardinals with the preposition *pa*, of.
   kue pa oghieñg
   the second thing.

*First* and *once* are represented by *pa-tagh*; *last* is represented by *pa-let*; *tagh* and *let* are also used for right and left.¹

#### PRONOUNS

##### PERSONAL PRONOUNS.

The full forms, generally used absolutely, or occasionally for the sake of emphasis, are:

| *Singular* | *Suk* | *Plural* | *Suk* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| I | ani. | we | acha. |
| thou | nyī.² | ye | akwa. |
| he, she, it | nyende,³ nye. | they | chane,³ cha. |

A slightly varied form of these is suffixed (or infixed) to verbs in a *subjective* or *objective* sense, or *both*.

*Ani* becomes *an* (if objective *anan*); *nyi* remains, but after a consonant becomes *enyi* (and if objective, *inyi*); *acha* and *akwa* are continually found as *echa* and *ekwa* (and if objective *acha* and *okwa*).

The third persons singular and plural, however, are not used as suffixes.⁴

The personal prefix in verbs is used laxly. This is probably the reason why a suffix (subjective) is employed.

When two vowels come together the first is elided:

> ¹ *Vide supra*, p. 65.
> ² Not to be confused with the demonstrative pronoun *nyĭ*, this.
> ³ *Nyende*, *chane* are the forms for 'he himself', 'they themselves' (*vide* p. 73). They are, however, much more frequently used as personal pronouns than *nye* and *cha*, which seldom appear.
> ⁴ But they do appear apparently in the two following phrases:—
>   tôma-nye kabkaʻ — he has not yet come.
>   tôma-che kabkaʻ — they have not yet come.
>   ani a-nye-ti — I am ready.
>   acha a-che-ti — we are ready.

*(Page 70)*

| *Suk* | *English* |
| :--- | :--- |
| chem-anan | love me! |
| o-chem-(akw-)acha | ye love us.¹ |
| ki-chem-(ech-)okwa | we love you.¹ |
| i-chem-enyi | thou lovest. |
| ki-chem-echa | we love. |
| *But:* chemei nyende | he loves him. |
| chemei chane² | they love them. |

Some of our people = pīch echa.

##### POSSESSIVE PRONOUNS.

Used predicatively or absolutely the forms of the possessive pronouns are:

| *English.* | *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| my | nyinyan³ | chechan. |
| thy | nyeñgmwunyi | chekunyi. |
| his, her, its | nyenyi | chechi. |
| ours | nyenja | checha. |
| yours | nyeñgmwa | chekwa. |
| theirs | nyeñgmwa | chekwa.⁴ |

When used in conjunction with nouns, shortened forms of the above are affixed:

| *English* | *Singular (after vowel / after cons.)* | *Plural (after vowel / after cons.)* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| my | -nyan, *after cons.* -inyan | -chan, *after cons.* -echan. |
| thy | -ñgmwu, *after cons.* -eñgmwu | -ku, *after cons.* -eku. |
| his, hers, its | -nyi, *after cons.* -inyi | -chi, *after cons.* -echi. |
| ours | -nja, *after cons.* -enja | -cha, *after cons.* -echa. |
| yours | -ñgmwa, *after cons.* -eñgmwa | -kwa, *after cons.* -akwa. |
| theirs | -ñgmwa, *after cons.* -eñgmwa | -kwa, *after cons.* -akwa. |

Examples:

| *English.* | *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| my article | kī-nyan | tuku'-chan. |
| thy article | kī-ngmwu | tuku-ku. |
| our article | kī-nja | tuku-cha. |

> ¹ In conversation when there is both a subjective and an objective suffix, the subjective infix is invariably dropped; e.g. ochem-acha, ye love us; ki-chem-akwa, we love you. Here the personal prefix is relied upon to show the subject of the sentence.
> ² As a matter of fact this expression would very rarely be used. The idiomatic way of expressing a phrase where the object is in the 3rd person singular or plural would be by the use of the dative form, q.v., pages 84, 85: *chem-chin-a*, love him!
> ³ The sing. and pl. prefixes in the absolute forms are conspicuous throughout, viz. *nye*, pl. *che*. Cf. Nandi.
> ⁴ It seems curious that the same words should do duty for both 2nd and 3rd persons plural, but such is, as far as I can make out, the case. When especial definiteness is required the demonstrative pronoun with the preposition *pa*, 'of,' is used: pa-pi'-chuno, theirs.

*(Page 71)*

| *English.* | *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| my spear | ñgôt-inyan | ngôtwa-chan. |
| our guinea fowl | mangarech-echa | mangarechoi-cha. |
| your (pl.) dog | kuki-kwa | kuki tin-akwa. |

##### DEMONSTRATIVE PRONOUNS.

There are three forms of demonstrative pronouns:
1. Those which denote objects near at hand.
2. Those which denote objects a little further off.
3. Those which denote objects some way off, out of sight.

| *English.* | *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| this | nyĭ | chu. |
| that (yet near) | nyino | chuno. |
| that (remote) | nyara | chara. |

Forms of the above are affixed to the nouns they qualify.
*chu* becomes *-chu* and after a consonant *-echu*.
*nyino* becomes *-nyino* (*-ñgino*, *-nino* rarely), and after a consonant *-ino*.
*chuno* becomes *-chuno*, and after a consonant *-echuno*.
*nyara* becomes *-nyara* (*-ñgara*, *-nara* rarely), and after a consonant *-ara*.
*chara* becomes *-chara*, and after a consonant *-echara*.

Examples:

| *Suk* | *English* |
| :--- | :--- |
| rotwoi-chu | these knives. |
| rotwa-nino | that knife (near). |
| rotwoi-chuno | those knives (near). |
| rotwa-nyara | that knife (remote). |
| rotwoi-chara | those knives (remote). |
| tai'-nyino | that cow near. |
| tai-nyara | those cows (near). |
| ōsis-w-echu¹ | these days. |
| konye'-chu² | these eyes. |
| mun'-echuno³ | those boys (near). |
| chemel-ino | that impala (near). |
| chemeltin-echara | those impala (remote). |
| wal-ôno | that place. |

*Chi* a man, pl. *pi*, *pich*, *pik*, takes slightly varied forms:

| *English.* | *Singular.* | *Plural.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| this man | chi-chi | pi'-chu. |
| that man (near) | chi-chino | pi'-chuno. |
| that man (remote) | chi-chara | pi'-chara. |

> ¹ Again the insertion of *w*, *vide supra*, p. 48.
> ² One would expect *konyen-echu*.
> ³ One would expect *munu'-chuno*.

*(Page 72)*

The above still leaves the singular demonstrative pronominal suffix *this* to be dealt with. It may be suffixed to the noun in the regular manner, as:
   akolopoin-nyi, this spoon.
   ñgotiñg-nyi, this lion.
   ñgeñg-(n)yi, this salt-lick.

but a suffix *-ei*, is very often employed instead:
   maiyos-ei, this monkey.
   chemel-ei, this impala.
   apiligaret-ei, this spur-fowl.
   asis-ei, this day.
   makal-ei, this ram.
   kel-ei, this leg.
   tokol-ei, this scorpion.
   mwagh-ei, this oil.
   kalambas-ei, this ant-bear.

But a very large number of words seem to have special forms of demonstrative suffixes of their own. A few examples are given here only, as the regular suffixes are always understood:
   rotwa-no, that knife [that knife (near) = rotwa-nino].
   koñg-ô, this eye.
   kutuñg-u, this hill.
   munuñg'-inge, this boy (munuñg = a boy).
   kiruk-i, this bull.
   mweghin-tono, that honey-barrel (subs. mweghin).
   sokon-tono, this leaf.
   pungus-iôno, that lizard.
   kepen-ôno, that cave.
   parei-yuno, that bed.
   mangarech-ani, this guinea-fowl.
   musuk-iôno, that pole.
   wuk-o, this hump.
   mochen-tone, that stick of sugar-cane.

##### REFLEXIVE PRONOUNS.

The word for 'self' is *tekegh* or *kegh*. To form a reflexive verb the above words are added:
   ka-mwut-an tekegh, I have cut myself.
   o-chem-an tekegh, I love myself.
or added as a suffix:
   ochema'-kegh, I love myself.
whereas ochem' ande, I myself love.

When the words 'myself', 'yourself', &c., are added to the personal pronouns, special words are used in Suk. These are formed from the personal pronouns with the suffix *de* in the singular and *ne* in the plural. The third persons singular and plural, however, are so often used simply as personal pronouns (*vide* p. 69, note 3) that for greater emphasis *nya-kegh*, pl. *cha-kegh*, are often added:

*(Page 73)*

| *English* | *Suk* |
| :--- | :--- |
| I myself | an-de. |
| thou thyself | nyin-de. |
| he himself | nyen-de¹ (nya-kegh). |
| we ourselves | acha-ne. |
| ye yourselves | ákwa-ne. |
| they themselves | cha-ne¹ (cha-kegh). |

Affixed to a verb these are:

| *Suk* | *English* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-chem'-ande | I myself love. |
| i-chem-enyinde | thou thyself lovest. |
| chemai nyende | he himself loves. |
| ki-chem-echane | we ourselves love. |
| o-chem-akwane | ye yourselves love. |
| chemei-chane² | they themselves love. |

To express *by myself*, *yourself*, &c., the pronouns *ande*, *nyinde*, &c., are strengthened by the addition of the word *ungit* in singular and *open* in plural:

| *English* | *Suk* |
| :--- | :--- |
| I by myself | ande-ungit. |
| he by himself | nyende-ungit. |
| you by yourselves | acha-ne-open. |
| they by themselves | chane-open.³ |
| we came by ourselves | ki-ki-pkon-echa-ne-open. |
| I have gone by myself | kowan ande-ungit. |

*Owner* is expressed by the word for 'man' with the preposition *pa*, of:
   chi-pa-kôt, the owner of the house.
   pi'-pa-tich, the owners of the cows.

##### RELATIVE PRONOUNS.

The relative pronoun is *nya* (less common *nyo*, *nyô*) in the singular and *cha* (less common *cho*, *chô*) in the plural:
   chi nya-karam, the man who is good.
   pi'-cha-karamech, the men who are good.

With the negative *ma* is inserted:
   chi nya-ma-karam, the man who is not good.
   pi'-cha-ma-karamech, the men who are not good.

> ¹ *Vide supra*, page 69, note 3.
> ² 'They love' simply would be *chem-ei*, only. The presence therefore of *chane* is sufficient to mark the distinction between 'they love' and 'they themselves love'.
> ³ Cf. Turkana *enyi-oopeny*.

*(Page 74)*

With verbs the ordinary negative is regularly employed:
   chi nya kow' amena, the man who went yesterday.
   chi nya ma-kowo-nye amena, the man who did not come yesterday.

##### INDEFINITE PRONOUNS.

As in Nandi, there are two indefinite pronouns. The first *anga*, plural *walak*,¹ other, another, different:
   chi anga, a different man; pl. pi'-walak.
   kuki-anga, a different dog; pl. kukitin walak.

'The one . . . the other' is translated by *anga . . . anga*:
   kowo anga ma-kowo-nye anga, one came another did not.

The second is *tokol* or *tukul*, each, every:
   chi-tokol, each man; pi-tokol, every man.
   But: pi-lopai, all men, 'every single man.'

*Both*, *all three*, &c., are translated by *kw'* before a vowel and *ko* before a consonant with the respective numerals preceded by tokol:
   tokol-kw'oghieñg, both; tokol-ko-mut, all five.

##### INTERROGATIVE PRONOUNS.

*Who?* and *which?* are translated by *ñgô*:
   ñgô-nyĭ? who is this? which is this?
   ñgô-chu? who are these? *or* which are these?
   po ñgô-nyi? whose is this? (lit. of whom is this?)

When *ñgô* is used objectively it follows the verb, otherwise it precedes it:
   i-ten-enyi-ñgô? to whom will you give it?

Used subjectively with a verb it requires the relative:
   ñgô nya mī kô, who is at the house?

*What?* = *nē*. This follows the verb, &c., except when used with a demonstrative pronoun:
   ne-nyĭ? what is this?
   i-mech-ei-ne? what do you want?

> ¹ Again the insertion of *w*. N. *alak*.

*(Page 75)*

*Of what sort?* is expressed by *kue-ne*¹:
   kue-ne-nyi? of what sort is this?

*How much?* *how many?* are translated by *ata*:
   pich-ata? how many men?

#### VERBS

##### REGULAR VERBS

Most transitive verbs are regular. Although imperfect and past perfect tenses exist, the present perfect most frequently does duty for both. This tense is occasionally used for the present, while the form of the present, again, does duty for the future. Contingent tenses may be formed by the present with an adverb, but they can also be expressed by the conditional tenses. There exist a present and a past conditional, but the former is often used for the latter, and vice versa. Indeed, for all practical purposes an acquaintance with the present, present perfect, one conditional, subjunctive, and imperative is all that is necessary. However, examples of all tenses are given below.²

It would appear that other tenses have dropped out, chiefly owing to the confusion in vowel-sounds existent in the Suk ear; a fact which has been emphatically pointed out in the beginning of this work.

Person is marked by pronominal prefixes and affixes in the first and second persons singular and plural. The affixes may be either subjective or objective.³ The subjective affixes, however, are not always employed. These are:

*I*, first person singular.
Prefix *o* in the present; and *o*, *ô*, or *ā*, preceded by the tense prefix, in the past tenses.

> ¹ By itself this expression *kue-ne* can mean 'for what reason?'—'why?'
> ² There would appear to be some further semi-obsolete tenses which do not occur in everyday conversation, e.g. a third conditional tense containing an extra past prefix and another tense with the prefix *to*, the value of which I have been unable to determine. I have thought better, in a work of investigation like the present, to omit such altogether rather than to insert what might afterwards prove to be incorrect. It must be remembered that at the time of writing there is not a single Suk in the country who understands the value of Suahili prefixes, and Suahili is the only medium at my command wherewith to delineate the various shades of tense meaning.
> ³ Discussed on p. 69, q.v.

*(Page 76)*

Subjective affix *-an*.
Objective affix *-anan*.
O-lim-an, I hear. Lim-anan! hear me! I-lim-anan, you hear me.

*Thou*, second person singular.
Prefix *i* in the present tense; in the past *e* preceded by the tense prefix.
Subjective affix *-enyi*.
Objective affix *-inyi*.
I-lim-enyi, you hear. O-lim-inyi, I hear you.

*We*, first person plural.
Prefix *ki* in the present and *ke* in the past tenses.
Subjective affix *-echa*.
Objective affix *-acha*.
Ki-lim-echa, we hear. O-lim-acha, you hear us.

*Ye*, second person plural.
Prefix *o* in both present and past tenses.
Subjective affix *-ekwa*.
Objective affix *-akwa* or *-okwa*.
O-lim-ekwa, we hear [ye hear]. Ki-lim-okwa, we hear you.

The third persons singular and plural take neither prefix nor affix. Where an objective affix is employed with the other persons the dative form¹ of the verb is used:
   He hears him, lim-jin.

##### THE RELATIVE.

The relative can be regarded as separate from the verb and need not be discussed further here. It is *nya* (rarely *nyo*, *-nyô*), or before a vowel *ny'*; and in the plural *cha* (rarely *cho*, *-chô*), or before a vowel *ch'*. Whether the relative is subjective or objective it occupies the same place, it being left to the verbal prefix to point out which is the case:
   ani ny' o-rip-an chi
   it is I who will follow the man.
   pi'-chuno cha ko-rep-an amena
   these are the men whom I followed last night.
   ani nya ka-rep chi-chino
   it was I whom that man followed.

##### *Present Tense.*

The sign of the present tense is the suffix *-ei* (rarely *-ai*) affixed to the root. When the personal subjective suffixes are used in the first

> ¹ Discussed on pp. 84, 85, q.v.

*(Page 77)*

and second persons singular and plural the tense suffix is dropped. Thus *I hear*, *you hear* may appear as *o-lim-ei*; *i-lim-ei*. When a present tense follows another present tense and has an infinitival signification it is the tense suffix and not the pronominal that invariably appears; e.g. o-mech-an o-lim-ei, I want to hear, lit. 'I want, I will hear.'

Sometimes both affixes are dropped and only the prefix retained. Thus o-lim, I hear.

| *Suk* | *English* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-lim-an (-ei) | I hear *or* am hearing. |
| i-lim-enyi (-ei) | thou hearest. |
| lim-ei | he hears. |
| ki-lim-echa (-ei) | we hear. |
| o-lim-ekwa (-ei) | ye hear. |
| lim-ei | they hear. |
| o-rip-an (-ei) | I follow *or* am following. |
| i-rip-enyi (-ei) | thou followest. |
| rip-ei | he follows. |
| ki-rip-echa (-ei) | we follow. |
| o-rip-ekwa (-ei) | ye follow. |
| rip-ei | they follow. |

The negative is formed by prefixing *m* + a vowel before a consonant and *m* before a vowel to the personal prefix, or in the case of the third persons singular and plural to the root, and suffixing *nye*.

This negative suffix, however, though never omitted in the first person singular, and rarely in the third persons singular and plural, is often omitted in the other persons either for the sake of brevity or euphony:

**I do not hear, &c., &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| m'o-lim-an-nye. | ma-ki-lim-echa (-nye). |
| m'i-lim-enyi (-nye). | m'o-lim-ekwa (-nye). |
| me-lim-ei (-nye). | me-lim-ei (-nye). |

##### *Future Tense.*

For the future tense the same form is used as for the present with the addition of some such word as asiyech, to-morrow, to mark the futurity; e.g.
   o-wet-an asiyech, I will come to-morrow.
   o-wet-an yē, I am coming now.

##### *Present Perfect.*

This is the most commonly used tense in Suk. Besides its present perfect signification, it is very frequently used instead of the past perfect, and occasionally even for the present tense. It is formed by prefixing

*(Page 78)*

*ka* (before a vowel *k*) to the personal prefixes of the first and second persons singular and plural, and to the root in the case of the third persons singular and plural. In this tense the personal prefix of the first person singular is found as *a*, *o*, or *ô*; that of the second person singular is *e*; and the first person plural *ke*.

In the third person singular and plural the tense prefix *ka* is sometimes found as *ke*:

| *I have heard, &c.* | *I have followed, &c.* |
| :--- | :--- |
| k-a-lim-an. | k-o-rep-an. |
| k-e-lim-enyi. | k-e-rep-enyi. |
| ka-lim.¹ | ka-rep.¹ |
| ka-ke-lim-echa. | ka-ke-rep-echa. |
| k-o-lim-ekwa. | k-o-rep-ekwa. |
| ka-lim. | ka-rep. |

The negative is formed by infixing *ma* (*m* before a vowel) between the tense prefix and the personal prefix or the root as the case may be, and affixing *nye*:

| *I have not heard, &c.* | *I have not followed, &c.* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ka-m-a-lim-an-nye. | ka-m-a-rep-an-nye. |
| ka-m-e-lim-enyi (-nye). | ka-m-e-rep-enyi (-nye). |
| ka-ma-lim (-nye). | ka-ma-rep (-nye). |
| ka-ma-ke-lim-echa (-nye). | ka-ma-ke-rep-echa (-nye). |
| ka-m-o-lim-ekwa (-nye).² | ka-m-o-rep-ekwa (-nye).² |
| ka-ma-lim (-nye). | ka-ma-rep (-nye). |

##### *Past Perfect.*

In ordinary conversation this tense is almost obsolete, but it frequently appears in stories and has the force of a narrative tense.

It is formed by prefixing *ki* in the same manner as in the present perfect.

In the third person singular and plural, however, the prefix frequently, but not invariably, appears as *kia*.

> ¹ A suitable vowel can always be added to third persons ending in consonants for the sake of euphony, e.g.
>   ka-rep-a pich, men followed.
> ² The subjective prefix does not always appear in this person; or perhaps it is changed to *a*.
>   ka-ma-lim-ekwa-nye,
>   ka-ma-rep-ekwa-nye,
> are sometimes heard. In which case possibly they stand for
>   ka-m-a-lim-ekwa-nye,
>   ka-m-a-rep-ekwa-nye.
> It is almost impossible to make a satisfactory rule for vowels in verb inflexions.

*(Page 79)*

| *I heard, &c.* | *I followed, &c.* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ki-a-lim-an. | ki-a-rep-an. |
| ki-e-lim-enyi. | ki-e-rep-enyi. |
| kia-lim. | kia-rep. |
| ki-ke-lim-echa. | ki-ke-rep-echa. |
| ki-o-lim-ekwa. | ki-o-rep-ekwa. |
| kia-lim. | kia-rep. |

In the negative form the prefix is *ki* throughout. The negative particle is infixed as in the preceding tenses.

**I did not hear, &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ki-ma-lim-an-nye. | ki-ma-ke-lim-echa (-nye). |
| ki-m'-e-lim-enyi (-nye). | ki-ma-lim-ekwa (-nye).¹ |
| ki-ma-lim (-nye). | ki-ma-lim (-nye). |

ki-ma-rep-an-ye, &c., I did not follow, &c.

##### *Imperfect.*

This tense, as in Nandi, is formed by the addition of the present perfect prefix *k* and a vowel to the form for the present tense. The second persons singular and plural, however, reduplicate the *k* sound so as to maintain the distinction from the corresponding persons of the present perfect. The tense is practically never used.²

**I was hearing, &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ka-o-lim-an (-ei). | ka-ki-ke-lim-echa (-ei). |
| ka-ke-lim-enyi (-ei). | ka-o-lim-ekwa (-ei). |
| ke-lim-ei. | ke-lim-ei. |

The negative is infixed after the past tense prefix:
   ka-m-o-lim-an-nye, I was not hearing.

##### CONDITIONAL MOOD.
###### *Present Tense.*

This tense, which can also be used as a present contingent tense, is formed by prefixing *atô* or, as it is often pronounced, *ata* to the root.

**If I hear . . . I shall. . . . Should I hear . . . I should . . . &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| atô-lim-an. | atô-ke-lim-echa. |
| atô-i-lim-enyi. | atô-lim-ekwa. |
| atô-lim. | atô-lim. |

The negative is formed by inserting the negative particle as before:

**If I do not hear . . . I shall not . . . *or* Should I not hear, &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| atô-ma-lim-an. | atô-ma-ke-lim-echa (-nye). |
| atô-me-lim-enyi. | atô-m-o-lim-ekwa (-nye). |
| atô-ma-lim. | atô-ma-lim (-nye). |

> ¹ Cf. *supra*, page 78, note 2.
> ² I have never heard it used in conversation.

*(Page 80)*

###### *Past Tense.*

This is formed by prefixing the past prefix *k* to the present conditional tense:

**If I had *or* should I have heard . . . I should have . . . &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| k-atô-lim-an. | k-atô-ke-lim-echa. |
| k-atô-e-lim-enyi. | k-atô-ke-lim-ekwa. |
| k-atô-lim. | k-atô-lim. |

*Negative*: **If I had not . . . I should not have . . . &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| k-atô-ma-lim-an-nye. | k-atô-ma-ke-lim-echa (-nye). |
| k-atô-m'e-lim-enyi (-nye). | k-atô-ma-lim-ekwa (-nye). |
| k-atô-ma-lim (-nye). | k-atô-ma-lim (-nye).¹ |

##### SUBJUNCTIVE MOOD.

There is one subjunctive tense which is formed by adding personal prefixes to the root of the verb. These prefixes are:
- 1st sing.: *a*
- 2nd sing.: *e*
- 1st pl.: *ke*
- 2nd pl.: *a*
- 3rd sing. and pl.: *ko*

This tense takes no personal affixes whatever:

**That I may hear, &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| a-lim. | ke-lim. |
| e-lim. | a-lim. |
| ko-lim. | ko-lim. |

The negative is formed by prefixing the *m* or *m* + a vowel to the personal prefixes.
Suffix *nye* is omitted.

**That I may not hear, &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| m-a-lim. | ma-ke-lim. |
| m-e-lim. | m-a-lim. |
| ma-ko-lim. | ma-ko-lim. |

##### INFINITIVE.

The infinitive is expressed in two ways:
1. By the present tense followed by the present tense with a future signification:
   o-mech-an o-lim-ei, I want, I will hear, i.e. I want to hear.

> ¹ There appears to be another conditional tense:
>   katô-*ka*-lim-an, &c.
> In the first part of a conditional sentence *anda* with the past perfect is used:
>   anda ka-lim katô-sich tainy.
>   had they heard they would have got a cow.

*(Page 81)*

2. By the use of the subjunctive:
   len-jin-a pi'cha-chañg a-pkan-a¹ a-lim ñgolion-inyan.
   tell many people to come and hear what I have to say.
   m-o-nget-an-nye a-par pan.
   I don't know how to divide the journey.

##### IMPERATIVE.

This is formed by suffixing *a* or *o* to the root in the singular and prefixing and suffixing *a* to the root in the plural:
   lim-a! hear thou!
   a-lim-a!² hear ye!
   kun-o cheghwa, give (me) some milk.

Personal objective suffixes may be employed:
   lim-anan!³ hear me!
   a-lim'-acha! hear ye us!

The present tense can be used in an imperative sense:
   m-i-weset-o-enyi, don't you go walking about;
and occasionally the subjunctive:
   a-lim, let me hear.

##### PASSIVE VOICE.

A verb is turned into the impersonal form or passive voice by prefixing *ko* to the root from which throughout all inflexions it henceforth becomes inseparable. The same affixes are employed, now of course all objective.

Root *muny*, to strike.

###### *Present Tense.*

| *Positive*: I am struck, &c. | *Negative*: I am not struck, &c. |
| :--- | :--- |
| komuny-anan. | ma-komuny-an(an)-nye. |
| komuny-inyi. | ma-komuny-inyi (-nye). |
| komuny-ei. | ma-komuny-ei (-nye). |
| komuny-acha. | ma-komuny-acha (-nye). |
| komuny-akwa. | ma-komuny-akwa (-nye). |
| komuny-ei. | ma-komuny-ei. |

> ¹ It will be noticed that there is in Suk no oratio obliqua. Thus the literal translation of this sentence is
>   'Tell many people—"come ye that ye may hear what I have to say".'
> ² This final *a* is often lengthened into *ô* or even *oi*; e.g.
>   len-jin-ô! / len-jin-a! / len-jin-oi! — tell him!
> ³ *Anan*. This is the reduplicated form of the first personal suffix as always used in the passive voice (*vide infra*).

*(Signature at foot: 1268 G)*

*(Page 82)*

###### *Past Tense.*

| *Positive*: I have been struck, &c. | *Negative*: I have not been struck, &c. |
| :--- | :--- |
| ka-komuny-anan. | ka-ma-komuny-an(an)-nye. |
| ka-komuny-inyi. | ka-ma-komuny-inyi (-nye). |
| ka-komuny.¹ | ka-komuny (-nye). |
| ka-komuny-acha. | ka-komuny-acha (-nye). |
| ka-komuny-akwa. | ka-komuny-akwa (-nye). |
| ka-komuny. | ka-komuny (-nye). |

###### *Conditional Present.*

| *Positive.* | *Negative.* |
| :--- | :--- |
| atô-komuny-anan. | atô-ma-komuny-an(an)-nye. |
| atô-komuny-inyi. | atô-ma-komuny-inyi (-nye). |
| atô-komuny. | atô-ma-komuny (-nye). |
| atô-komuny-acha. | atô-ma-komuny-acha (-nye). |
| atô-komuny-akwa. | atô-ma-komuny-akwa (-nye). |
| atô-komuny. | atô-ma-komuny (-nye). |

###### *Conditional Past.*

   k-atô-komuny-anan, &c.
   If I had been struck *or* Should I have been struck . . . I should, &c.

   *Negative.* k-atô-ma-komuny-an(an)-nye.
   If I had not been struck *or* Should I not have been struck . . . I should, &c.

##### SUBJUNCTIVE.

For this tense *ke* is prefixed to the root and remains inseparable from it.

**That I may be struck, &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| kemuny-anan. | kemuny-acha. |
| kemuny-inyi. | kemuny-akwa. |
| kemuny-ei. | kemuny-ei. |

*Negative*: **That I may not be struck, &c.**

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ma-kemuny-anan. | ma-kemuny-acha. |
| ma-kemuny-inyi. | ma-kemuny-akwa. |
| ma-kemuny-ei. | ma-kemuny-ei. |

Infinitive and imperative passive do not appear to exist.

> ¹ *Komuny* is of two syllables only, and somewhat difficult of pronunciation.

*(Page 83)*

#### COMPLEX VERBS

##### Affix *oi*.

Some verbs take an affix *oi* between the root and the personal affix. Possibly this affix originally denoted motion from the speaker, but its signification has grown wider. There is nothing to distinguish these verbs from simple verbs except in present tense indicative and the imperative.

###### PRESENT INDICATIVE. I escape from, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-tiok-oi-an. | ki-tiok-oi-echa. |
| i-tiok-oi-enyi. | o-tiok-oi-ekwa. |
| tiok-oi. | tiok-oi. |

###### PRESENT PERFECT. I have escaped from, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ka-tiak-an. | ka-ki-tiak-echa. |
| ke-tiak-enyi. | k-o-tiak-'wa.¹ |
| ka-tiak. | ka-tiak. |

###### IMPERATIVE.
   tiok-ena, pl. a-tiok-ena.

Other examples of the 'motion from' sense are:
   o-chint-oi-an, I fall *from* above.
   o-tiart-oi-an, I drop (act) *from* above.
   o-wirt-oi-an, I fling *from* me.

But the affix also denotes rest at:
   o-tep-oi-an, I rest *at*.

And again:
   o-ruwatat-oi-an, I dream *about*.
   o-wutioʻ-oi-an, I forget *about*.

And compare:
   o-sich-an, I obtain.
   o-sik-oi-an, I obtain *for*.

Also:
   o-ghomit-oi-an, I collect *together*.
   o-kuot-oi-an, I crawl *along*.

So it appears that the affix *oi* practically corresponds to any preposition which in English is connected with the verb, but seeing that there is no affix whereby to distinguish any tense other than the present indicative and the imperative mood, it seems justifiable to regard this affix as a mere reminiscence of Nandi and as having no significant value.

> ¹ Note usual avoidance of reduplication.

*(Signature at foot: G 2)*

*(Page 84)*

#### RECIPROCAL VERBS

Affix *soi* in the present and affix *i* in the past tenses are the distinctive features of these verbs.
3rd sing. and pl. pres. end in final *sut*.
3rd sing. and pl. past end in final *ioʻ*.
Imperatives are formed by affixing *iena* to the stem in the singular, and by adding a further prefix *a* to form the plural.

###### PRESENT TENSE. I fight with a person, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-pôr-soi-an. | ki-pôr-soi-echa. |
| i-pôr-soi-enyi. | o-pôr-soi-ekwa. |
| pôr-sut. | pôr-sut. |

###### PRESENT PERFECT. I have fought with, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ko-pôr-i-an. | ka-ki-pôr-i-echa. |
| ke-pôr-i-enyi. | k-o-pôr-i-ekwa. |
| ke-pôr-ioʻ. | ke-pôr-ioʻ. |

###### IMPERATIVE.
   pôr-iena, pl. a-pôr-iena.

Other examples of reciprocal verbs are:

| *English* | *Present* | *Past Perfect* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| I argue or disagree with | o-ti-soi-an | p.p. kô-ti-i-an. |
| I meet with | o-nyôr-soi-an | p.p. ko-nyôr-i-an. |

*Exceptions.*
   I cross a river: o-gh-soi-an, p.p. ka-ogh-an.
   I sleep, *o-ruw-an*, in the present indicative, takes affix *soi* in the plural only (*vide* page 88), and has certainly no reciprocal meaning.

##### THE DATIVE FORM.

This is practically the same as in Nandi.
The mark of the present tense is affix *chin* or *jin*, while that of the past is *chi* or *ji* or *ch* or *j*.

###### *Present Tense*: I tell (to) a person.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-mwô-chin-an. | ki-mwô-chin-echa. |
| i-mwô-chin-enyi. | o-mwô-chin-ekwa. |
| mwô-chin. | mwô-chin. |

*Or*, with the same meaning:

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-len-jin-an. | ki-len-jin-echa. |
| i-len-jin-enyi. | o-len-jin-ekwa. |
| len-jin. | len-jin. |

###### *Present Perfect*: I have told to a person.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ka-mwô-ch-an. | ka-ki-mwô-chi-cha. |
| ke-mwô-ch-inyi. | ko-mwô-chi-kwa. |
| ka-mwô-chi. | ka-mwô-chi. |

*(Page 85)*

The *imperative* as usual is formed from the present tense:
   mwô-chin-a, pl. a-mwô-chin-a.
   mwô-chin-anan *or* len-jin-anan, tell me.

Other examples of the dative form are:
   o-rôr-chin-an, I laugh *at* a person.
   o-rem-jin-an, I assist a person (i.e. give help *to*).
   o-tiôr-chin-an, I look fixedly *at* a person.

But 'I change' (intrans.), *o-wel-jin-an*, can hardly be considered an instance of the dative form.

Dative forms can always be coined so as to do away with the necessity of employing a personal pronoun when the object is in the third person singular.
Thus: he loves him, *chem-chin*, instead of *chemei nyende*.

##### QUASI-PASSIVE FORM.

This is common, verbs being made from other verbs, adjectives, and adverbs by infixing *itun* in the present and *itu* in the past tenses. This is entirely different from Nandi.

Adj. *terus*, fat.

###### *Present Tense*: I become fat.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-terus-itun-an. | ki-terus-itun-echa. |
| i-terus-itun-enyi. | o-terus-itun-ekwa. |
| terus-itun. | terus-itun. |

###### *Present Perfect* (and past perfect also).

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ka-terus-itu-an. | ka-ki-terus-itu-echa. |
| ke-terus-itu-nyi. | ko-terus-itu-ekwa. |
| ka-terus-itu. | ka-terus-itu. |

*Imperative*: terus-itun-a, pl. o-terus-itun-a.
o-legit-itun-an, I become near, i.e. I draw nigh.

##### Affix *un* and *in*.

These appear to be intransitive affixes, and for the most part express motion.

| *Present.* | *English.* | *Past.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| o-chun-un-an | I fall like rain | ka-chun-u-an. |
| o-t-un-an | I come in | ko-t-u-an. |
| o-let-un-an | I come behind | ko-let-u-an. |
| o-legit-un-an | I come near | ka-legit-u-an. |
| o-kut-un-an | I boil (int.) | ko-kut-u-an. |
| o-nyur-un-an | I assemble (int.) | ka-nyur-u-an. |
| o-pet-in-an | I fall into | ko-pet-i-an. |

Yet, if indeed they are intransitive affixes, there are a large number of exceptions, as

| *Present.* | *English.* | *Past.* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| o-k-in-an | I heat | ko-k-i-an. |
| o-iyuk-un-an | I give back | ka-iyuk-u-an. |

*(Page 86)*

##### Affix *it* inseparable from the verb.

By this affix intransitive verbs may be made transitive.

| *Intransitive Meaning* | *Root* | *Transitive Meaning* | *Transitive Form* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| to fear | root wegh | I frighten | o-wegh-it-an. |
| to get well | root sop | I cure | o-sop-it-an. |
| to be lost | root pot | I lose | o-pot-it-an. |
| to become full | root nyegh | I fill | o-nyegh-it-an. |

##### Affix *isi* expresses continuity.

| *Verb* | *Root* | *Simple Form* | *Continuous Form* | *Meaning* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| to sell | root l | o-l-an, I sell | o-l-isi-an | I trade. |
| to eat | root m | o-m-an, I eat | o-m-isi-an | I take food. |
| to practise witchcraft | root pen | o-pen-an | o-pen-isi-an | I practise witchcraft continually. |

#### IRREGULAR VERBS

##### **To be.** *Present.*

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| I am, o-cha. | we are, ki-chi-cha. |
| thou art, oi-chi. | ye are, o-chi-kwa. |
| he is, —. | they are, —. |

###### *Past.*

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| I was, ki-ocha. | we were, ki-ki-chicha. |
| thou wast, ki-ichi. | ye were, kio-chikwa. |
| he was, ki-chi. | they were, ki-chi. |

*Imperative wanting.*

##### **To be present.** Root *mit*.

###### *Present*: I am present, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-mīt-an. | ki-mit-echa. |
| i-mit-enyi. | o-mit-ekwa. |
| mi, mit-o. | mi, mit-o. |

###### *Negative*: I am not present, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| m-o-mit-an-nye. | ma-ki-mit-echa (-nye). |
| m-i-mit-enyi (-nye). | m-o-mit-ekwa (-nye). |
| ma-mi-nye.¹ | ma-mi-cha (-nye). |

Rarely mo-mit-o-nye, third pers. sing. and pl.

###### *Present Perfect*: I have been present, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| k-o-mit-an. | ki-ko-mit-echa. |
| k-e-mit-enyi. | k-o-mit-ekwa. |
| ko-mi-to. | ko-mi-to. |

> ¹ This suffix is never omitted.

*(Page 87)*

###### *Negative*: I have not been present, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ka-m-o-mit-an-ye. | ka-mo-ki-mit-echa (-nye). |
| ka-m-e-mit-enyi (-nye). | ka-m-o-mit-ekwa (-nye). |
| ka-mo-mit-o (-nye). | ka-mo-mit-o (-nye). |

##### **To see.** Root *sow*.

###### *Present*: I see, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-sowiy-an. | ki-sowiy-echa. |
| i-sowiy-enyi. | o-sowiy-ekwa. |
| sowiy-ī. | sowiy-i. |

###### *Past*: I have seen, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ka-sow-an. | ka-ki-sow-echa. |
| ke-sow-enyi. | ka-sow-ekwa. |
| ka-sow-a. | ka-sow-a. |

###### *Imperative.*
   sowena! see thou! | a-sow-ena! see ye!

##### **To go.** Roots *wet*, *w* (sing.), *pet* (pl.).

###### *Present*: I go, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-wet-an *or* o-w-an. | ki-pet-echa. |
| i-wet-enyi *or* i-w-enyi. | o-pet-ekwa. |
| wet-ei. | pet-ei. |

###### *Past perfect*: I went, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ki-ow-an. | ki-ke-pet-echa. |
| ki-ow-enyi. | ki-o-pet-ekwa. |
| ki-ow-a. | ki-pet-ei. |

###### *Imperative.*
   wiy-a! go! | a-pe! go ye!

##### **To come.** Roots *mwun* (sing.), *pkon* (pl.).

###### *Present*: I come, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-mwun-an. | ki-pkon-echa. |
| i-mwun-enyi. | o-pkon-ekwa. |
| mwun-ei. | pkon-ei. |

###### *Past perfect*: I came, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ki-au-mwun-an. | ki-ki-pkon-echa. |
| ki-au-mwun-enyi. | ki-o-pkon-ekwa. |
| ki-au-mwun. | ki-pkon-ei. |

###### *Present perfect*: I have come, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| k-o-mwun-an. | ka-ki-pkon-echa. |
| k-e-mwun-enyi. | k-o-pkon-ekwa. |
| ke-mwun. | kabkaʻ. |

*(Page 88)*

###### *Imperative.*
   nyunu! (or nyun-ô) come! | a-pkan-a! come ye!

##### **To sleep.** Root *ruw*.

###### *Present*: I sleep, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-ruw-an. | ki-ruw-soi-echa. |
| i-ruw-enyi. | o-ruw-soi-ekwa. |
| ruw-ei. | ruw-ei. |

###### *Past*: I slept, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| ki-o-ruw-an. | ki-ke-ruwi-echa. |
| ki-i-ruw-enyi. | ki-o-ruwi-ekwa. |
| ki-ruw-ei. | ki-ruw-ei. |

###### *Imperative.*
   ruw-a! sleep! | a-ruwien-a! sleep ye!

##### **To know.** Root *nget*.

###### *Present*: I know, &c.

| *Singular* | *Plural* |
| :--- | :--- |
| o-nget-an. | ki-nget-echa. |
| i-nget-enyi. | o-nget-ekwa. |
| ingit. | ingit. |

The *past perf. tense* is regular but for the third persons singular and plural, which are *ki-engit*.

###### *Imperative.*
   ngit-a! know! | a-ngit-a! know ye!

##### **To give.** Root *ten*.

This verb is only irregular in the third persons singular and plural of the present:
   ten-a, he or they give.
   keta, he or they gave.

###### *Imperative.*
   kuno! give! | o-kuno! give ye!

#### REDUPLICATION

There are a certain number of instances of reduplication, signifying emphasis or reiteration; e.g.
   o-mut-an, I cut.
   o-mun-mut-an, I cut to pieces.
   r. sach: o-sosoch-an, I shake *up* (e.g. milk to make butter).
   But: o-iyer-an, I search.
   o-iyer-an nyaman, I will search thoroughly.

*(Page 89)*

#### AUXILIARY VERBS

**To be able**, root *mwuch*, is followed by the present tense indicative:
   o-mwuch-an o-wet-ei, I am able to go.

*Must* can only be expressed by emphasis on the last syllable and can be strengthened by the addition of *nyaman*:
   o-wet-án *or* owetán nyaman, I must go.

#### ADVERBS

Adverbs follow the verbs they qualify:
   ñgolon-a môt! speak slowly!
*slowly*, môt: môt-môt-môt, very slowly.
*for nothing*, kuliʻ: kokomunyan kuli, I was beaten for nothing.
*before*, tagh.
*behind*, lĕt.
*above*, yĭm: yim-ô-kô, above the house.
*below*, ñgwen: ñgwen-ô-lalua, below the river.
*near*, légit.
*far*, lōh (also an adj.).
*quickly*, lawel.
*early*, tokwagha (early this morning, *akai*).
*at even*, mĕnun.

By prefixing *kio* adjectives become adverbs:
   kio-meneñg, little.

*Man*: *nya-man* corresponds to the Nandi *mising*, and can be used as many adverbs in English. It actually means indeed, very much so, as cf. the Suahili *sana*:
   wiy-a nya-man! go hard!
   lim-a nya-man! hear well!

##### ADVERBS OF TIME AND COMPOUND WORDS USED ADVERBIALLY.

*now*, yē or suffix *-ei*, this: asis-ei, to-day.
*instantly*, lawel-lawel.
*lately*, oghi-tagh; lit. before the day before yesterday.
*presently*, tôma-nye kio-meneng.
*before*, tôma.
*not yet*, tôma-nye¹ (tôma-che).¹
*ago*, kainy-isioʻ.
*long ago*, kainy-man; lit. time indeed, *vide supra* nya-man.

> ¹ *Vide* p. 69, n. 4.

*(Page 90)*

*afterwards*, *-yă* suffixed.
*to-morrow*, asiyēch.
*day after to-morrow*, kaiy'-oghi.
*yesterday evening*, amena: omatu, yesterday.
*day before yesterday*, oghi.
*always*, ōsis-tokol, each day.
*every day*, ñgat.
*these days*, osis-w-echu.

##### ADVERBS OF PLACE.

There is a prefix *ole*, *ol'*, which is identical with the Nandi *ole* meaning 'where'. This is inseparable from the verb:
   ongetan ol'-i-wet-eny' 'uno. — I know where you are going.
   wiy-a ole-mi pi'-w-alak. — go where the others are.

##### ADVERBS OF MANNER.

*like this*, le-nyô-nyi; lata-nyô-nyi.
*only*, bat.
*indeed*, *very*, man; nya-man.
*no*, ērwoʻ (emphatic erwoʻ-woʻ-woʻ-woʻ-woʻ).
*yes*, ō-wei.
*together*, tukul-okoñgô.

##### INTERROGATIVE ADVERBS.

*how?* le-ne? in what manner? lata-ne?
*where?* ngō?¹ ngo pi' chuno? where are those men?
*whither?* nyuno? (affixed to verb in 2nd pers. sing.) 'uno i-wet-eny'-uno? where are you going?
*why?* mbĕ-ne? (lit. 'because of what?').
*how many?* ata?
*when?* ôyu?
*since when?* ñget-isio-oyu?

Examples:
   how shall we go? o-pet-ekwa le-ne?
   where is Logwomeri? ngo Logwomeri?
   whence came you? ki-au-mwuny-eny² 'uno?
   how many men have come? kabkaʻ pich'-ata?
   how long since he has come? ñgetisioʻ-oyu ki-au-mwun?
   when did he come? ki-au-mwun ôyu?

> ¹ Distinguish *ngo*? where? *ñgô*, who, and *ngô*, and.
> ² *Vide supra*.

*(Page 91)*

#### CONJUNCTIONS

*and*, *with*, ngô.
*again*, ñgat.
*either*, *or*, eku . . . eku.
*before*, tôma.
*for*, mba, mbe.
*because of*, mba, mbe.
*but*, walô.
*if* = anda, and is also expressed by the conditional tense.

#### PREPOSITIONS

*of*, pa, or if speaking of relationship, to. *at*, *to*, and *in* are not expressed.
   mi-to¹ lalua, he is at the river.
   mi kô, he is in his house.
   ki-ki-nyuru-echa wū, we found it in the wood.
*about*, ñgala (pa): lugoi ñgala (pa) munuñg, news about the boy.
*with*, ngô. It is also expressed by a most clumsy periphrasis: he came with me, kiaumwun ki-ki-pkon-echa tukul; lit. he came—we have come—each.
*by*, nga: kokomuny nga 'ni, he was hit by me.
*for*, ka; but very seldom.

#### INTERJECTIONS

Greeting a chief is accompanied by an elaborate collection of expressions in which the root *chamke* appears evident. A visitor on entering the elder's hut is addressed first by the chief. The following is a specimen. It is introduced by the preliminary word *Tokwes*, greeting.
   *Elder.* Chamke-t!² Health!
   *Visitor.* Chamke-t! Health!
   *Elder.* Chamke-cha! Health to us!
   *Visitor.* Chamke-cha! Health to us!
   *Elder.* Chamke-kau! Health to your village!
   *Visitor.* Chamke-sapon! Good health! (Health to your health!)
   *Elder.* Chamke-rurwa! Health in your shade trees!
   *Visitor.* Chamke-cheghwa! Health from the milk you drink!
   *Elder.* Chamke-cha! Health to us!

The whole of the above will be repeated in turn even if there be fifty or more visitors. The ordinary greeting however is:
   To a man: subai! suboi! *Answer*: subai! *or* ērpa!
   To a woman: tapkwenya! *Answer*: īgo!
Other forms of address have been discussed under the vocative case.

> ¹ Perhaps this is a locative suffix *-to*.
> ² *Chamke* is a Nandi verb root signifying to be in good health. The word does not occur in Suk except in this greeting.

*(Page 92)*

| *Meaning* | *Interjection* |
| :--- | :--- |
| of astonishment | hô! |
| acknowledgement of call | i! hoi! yoi! |
| of assent | ô!; ō-wei! |
| acknowledgement of veracity | man! nya-man! |
| of disgust | het! (accompanied by averting the head). |
| of contempt | pfer! |
| of grief | woi-ye! |
| of joy | very lengthened ô . . .! |
| of surprise | hei-wei! |
| of farewell | korôton! *Answer*: i-rôt-enyi! (lit. I have gone! Thou art going). |
| of doubt | ĕso = who knows? |

Also imperatives as:
   nam-a! take hold! | lĭm-a! listen!
   sis-a! silence! | pist-ena! leave it!
   ros-a! look!

#### SOME USEFUL AND IDIOMATIC PHRASES

- **I-lend-ei-ne? / I-lend-enyi-ne?** — What do you say?
  *Lit.* You-saying-are-what?
- **Ka-len-j-i nyi ne?** — What did he say to you?
  *Lit.* (He) has-said-to-you-what?
- **Mwô-w-anan. / Mwô-chin-anan.** — Tell me.
  *Lit.* Tell-to-me.
- **Me-t-ei-nye oror oghieñg.** — It will not be as much as two months.
  *Lit.* It not-sufficient-is-not months two.
- **Mba ki-'no kô-nonsi-a' ka-legit-itu luk.** — That is why I thought the battle was imminent.
  *Lit.* For thing that did-I-think-I it had-near-become the-battle.
- **Me kue a-iyit.** — It is no good me counting.
  *Lit.* Not anything that I-count.
- **Me-nget-nye ekw'-oyu.** — He doesn't know when.
  *Lit.* He not-knows-not when.
- **Ka-kut-a¹ songol ôr.** — They put an ostrich-feather in the road.
  *Lit.* He has-put ostrich feather road.
- **Anda ka-sow-a' blerkwa katô-m-o-wegh-an-nye.** — Had I seen the hare I should not have been afraid.
  *Lit.* If I-have seen-I the-hare should have-not-I-feared-I-not.

> ¹ The addition of the vowel is always permissible for the sake of euphony.

*(Page 93)*

- **Atô-ke-cham.** — If he likes.
- **Ki-au-mwun atô-k¹-ômisi-an.** — He came while I was eating.
  *Lit.* He-did-come if-?-I am eating-I.
- **Mbe-ne k-e-gh-enyi ki 'no?** — What did you do that for?
  *Lit.* For-what have-you-done-you thing that?
- **Wiy-a len-jin-a o-mech-inyi.** — Go and tell him I want him.
  *Lit.* Go tell-to-him 'I-want-you'.
- **Ka-ñget-an o-muny ko-loich** — I got up and hit (him) and he cried.
  *Lit.* I did-get up-I I-hit so that he-cries.
- **I-gh-enyi-ne?** — What are you doing?
  *Lit.* You-are doing-you-what?
- **M-o-nget-an-nye eku ôr-ne.** — I don't know where (he) went to.
  *Lit.* Not-I-know-I-not whether road-what.
- **O-nget-an a-iyit atô-mwun-ei ekw' oyu.** — I can reckon when it will come.
  *Lit.* I-know-I how I may-count if-coming-he is or when.
- **Ma-mwa-nye nyino.** — He didn't say so.
  *Lit.* Not he-says-not that.
- **Kun-anan pan o-woʻ.** — Give me leave to go.
  *Lit.* Give-me a journey that I may-go.
- **Ocha karam ma-mi-nye.** — I am the best of all.
  *Lit.* I-am good there not-is-not (any better).
- **Per-ana' met.** — My head aches.
  *Lit.* It is beating me-the head.
- **M-o-nget-an-nye a-par pan.** — I don't know how to divide the journey.
  *Lit.* Not-I-know-I-not how I may divide the journey.
- **Atô-ke-sop kel.** — If my leg has got well.
  *Lit.* If-it has-got well the leg.
- **Tep-ena ñgmwen!** — Sit down!
  *Lit.* Sit-you at the bottom.

> ¹ This *k* is probably merely an euphonetic connective.

*(Page 94)*

- **Ki-au-mwun amenaʻ kuam-chi, ke-ñget obesu pich, ke-par ke-par ka-maʻ.** — Last night came a beast of prey, men got up early next morning and speared and speared it until it was dead.
  *Lit.* He-came last night the man-devourer, they-got up early the next day men, they have-struck they have-struck, it has-died.
- **O-tet-oi-a' rupia-tin cha-pa-kelat.** — I will give rupees for the tusks.
  *Lit.* I-will give-for-I rupees which (are)-of-the tusks.
- **Ka-m-o-lim-j-an-nye.** — I did not hear him.
  *Lit.* Have-not-I-hear-him-I-not.
- **Ma-ka-maʻ-nye.** — He did not die.
  *Lit.* Not-he did-die-not.
- **Ros-anan eku o-nget-an.** — See if I know.
  *Lit.* Try-me if I-know-I.
- **O-mech-an o-tit-oi . . .** — I want to reach.
  *Lit.* I-want-I I-will arrive-at.
- **Atô-ke-cham ekw' ô-l-ei.** — If they want to buy.
  *Lit.* If-they-like whether they-buying-are.
- **M-o-nget-an-nye eku rupia-tin ata.** — I don't know how much they will fetch.
  *Lit.* Not-I-know-I-not whether rupees how many.
- **Eku ñgolôn-ei cha-karam-ech eku cha-gha-ch.** — Either will he speak nicely or otherwise.
  *Lit.* Or say-he-will what-are-nice or what-are-bad.
- **Ki-chiren-echa ñgat.** — We must converse daily.
  *Lit.* We-will converse-we daily.
- **O-mech-a' o-wet-ei Kerut.** — I want to go to Kerut.
  *Lit.* I-want-I I-am going Kerut.
- **Ki-ruwok ô let.** — The consultation shall be afterwards.
- **Ki-lend-e'-ne¹ asiyech?** — What shall we do to-morrow?
  *Lit.* We-shall do-what to-morrow?

> ¹ r. *lend* = say. But in this particular phrase, where *ki* is an interrogative particle rather than a pronominal prefix, it is always used in the sense of *do*.

*(Page 95)*

- **Kerio wal-nya-pôro.** — The lower reaches of the Kerio river.
- **Tērp-a nyende. / Tērp-jin-a.** — Ask him.
- **Ka-tagh me-mwun-ei-nye.** — He refused to come.
  *Lit.* He has-refused not he-is coming-not.
- **Tukul-cha-bat!** — That's all!
  *Lit.* All-which-is-only.
- **Kō-lan-ô / Kō-lan-oi / Kô-lan-ô** — 'I said' (in prefacing a sentence).
- **Me-ghaʻ-nye.¹** — It is not bad.
  *Lit.* Not-bad-it.
- **Tôma-ny'-'abkaʻ.²** — He has not yet come. (*Lit.* Not yet-he-has come.)
- **Tôma-ch'-'abkaʻ.²** — They have not yet come. (*Lit.* Not yet-they-have come.)
- **Me-mwun-ei-nye.** — He won't come.
- **Pi'-chuno anda ka-lim katô-ka-sich tainy.** — Had those people heard they would have got a cow.
  *Lit.* People-those if they have-heard would have-they have-got a cow.
- **Atô-chem-ei nyende. / Atô-chem-jin.** — If he likes him.
- **Me-t-ei-nye taman.** — They don't amount to ten.
  *Lit.* Not-amount-do-not ten.
- **I-keñg-enyi-ne?** — What are you waiting for?
- **Lata-chô-nyi ñgal-echi.** — Such were his words.
  *Lit.* Like-what-is-this words-his.
- **Mi Chambus semiyu.** — There is cattle sickness at En-jemus.
- **M-o-non-an-nye.** — I don't remember.
- **M-e-sich-enyi(-nye).** — You can't have it.
- **Ke-gh le-ny'-etei.** — He did it like this.
  *Lit.* He-did like-what is-here.
- **Karam kochiyu.** — Excellent!

> ¹ Here *nye* is the 3rd sing. pronoun, not the negative affix, *vide* page 69, note 4.
> ² Note these instances of arbitrary elision. The most important part of the verb, viz. the tense prefix *ka*, is dropped out merely to facilitate expression.

*(Page 96)*

- **O-mer-a' nyende ko-mwun.** — I want him to come.
  *Lit.* I-want-I him that he may-come.
- **Wiy-a ros-a!** — Go and see!
- **A-mal-a kegh!** — Out of the way you! (pl.)
- **K-e-gh-enyi le-ne?** — How did you do it?
- **K-a-sow-a' kuliʻ.** — I saw (it was) empty.
- **Lata-ne? . . . Karam.** — How are you? . . . Quite well.
- **Mbe-ne se¹-me-ñgalan ñgala chuno pa-tagh?** — Why didn't you say so at first?
  *Lit.* For-what ?-not-you-said words those of-in front?
- **Nyur-o se¹-kebe tukul.** — Let us come together.
  *Lit.* Come-you ?-forward together.
- **O-nyur-un-inyi asiyech.** — I will catch you up to-morrow.
  *Lit.* I-will meet-with(?)-you to-morrow.
- **Kebe!** — Forward! Let us be going.
- **Pogh-po-maʻ.** — Hot water. (*Lit.* Water-of-fire.)
- **Sera!** — Thank-you!
- **Ki-lend-e-ne kainat-nyeñgmwunyi?** — What is your name?
- **Ka-kowir-inyi kel kogh?** — Did you hit your foot against a stone?
  *Lit.* Has-there-been-a-beating-with respect to you (on your) foot (by) a stone?
- **Ka-ki-nyuru-echa kainy.** — We have met before.
- **K-e-ten-eny' 'uno?** — Where have you put it?
- **Ki-au-mwun oyu?** — When did he come?
- **Me-mi-chi-nye.** — It is insufficient.
- **Ke-mi-chi-nyi-ne?** — What is this worth?
- **O-mer-a' nya mwañgwa.** — I want more.
- **Rir-ei oyu omisioʻ?** — When will food be ready?
- **Ka-komuny-nyende.** — He was beaten by him.
- **Mi-ne?** — What is the matter?

> ¹ I have been unable to determine the value of this prefix *se*.

*(Page 97)*

- **Ki-ow-an wal-ô-mi-nye.** — I went in his stead: *lit.* in the place where he is.
- **O-sik-oi-a' tainy nyeñgmwunyi.** — I will get you a cow.
  *Lit.* I-will get-for-I (or I-get-for-I) a cow yours.

| Suk | tich | ekw' | akwa | walô | ki-pet-echa | ne-open |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| *Gloss* | cattle | or | you | but | we-will-go-we | by-ourselves |

| Suk | ngô | ki-ghut-echa | lata | ny'anga |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| *Gloss* | and | we-will-bleat-we | (in a) way | that-is-different. |

## VOCABULARY

*(Page 99)*

THE root, 1st pers. sing. of the present and present perfect tenses, are given in this sequence. The following abbreviations are employed:—

| Abbreviation | Meaning | Abbreviation | Meaning |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| irreg. | = irregular. | intr. | = intransitive. |
| pr. | = present indic. | adv. | = adverb. |
| r. | = root. | s. | = substantive. |
| p.p. | = pres. perf. | Nand. | = Nandi. |
| m. | = masculine. | Turk. | = Turkana. |
| f. | = feminine. | sing. | = singular. |
| adj. | = adjective. | pl. | = plural. |
| v. | = verb. | lit. | = literally. |
| trans. | = transitive. | | |

### A

- **A what-is-it**, kue, ki, pl. tukun.
  *So and So*, anem, kam-ne.
- **Abdomen**, mū, pl. mu-tin.
- **Abhor, to**: r. sas (*vide* **Hate**).
- **Able, to be**: r. mwuch, pr. o-mwuch-an, -ei, p.p. ka-mwuch-an.
- **Abort, to**. *Of cattle*: r. tōru, p.p. 3rd sing. ka-tōru.
  *Of women*: r. rupu, pr. o-rupu-an, p.p. ka-rupu-an.
- **Abound, to**. *Cows abound*, mito ti' cha-chang.
  *Make full, to*: r. nyeghit, pr. o-nyeghit-an, -ei, p.p. ka-nyeghit-an.
- **About** (*near*), lĕgit.
- **Above**, yĭm; yim-ô-kô, *above the house*.
- **Abscess**, tasôs, pl. tesus.
- **Abundantly**, nya-man, man.
- **Abuse, to**: r. mwagh, pr. o-mwogh-an, -ei, p.p. ka-mwagh-an.
- **Accept, to**: r. cham, pr. o-chem-an, -ei, p.p. ka-cham-an.
  *To receive*: r. tăch, pr. o-tóch-an, -ei, p.p. ka-tăch-an (Nand. *id.*).
- **Accompany, to**: r. iyomis, pr. o-iyomis-an, -ei, p.p. ko-iyomis-an.
- **Accustomed, to be**: r. nagh, pr. o-negh-an, -ei, p.p. ka-nagh-an.
- **Ache, to**: r. chir, pr. o-chir-an, -ei, p.p. ka-chir-an.
  *My head aches*, per-ana' met.
- **Add, to**: r. tas, pr. o-tes-an, -ei, p.p. ka-tas-an.
- **Admire, to**: r. cham, pr. o-chem-an, -ei, p.p. ka-cham-an.
- **Adorn, to**: r. iyin, pr. o-iyin-an, -ei, p.p. ka-iyin-an.
- **Adultery, to commit**: r. kum, pr. o-kum-an, -ei, p.p. ko-kum-an.
- **Advance, to**: r. tôwōgh, pr. o-tōwōgh-an, p.p. kô-tôwōgh-an.
- **Advice**, ki-ruwok, pl. ki-ruwok-ai.
- **Advise, to**: r. ruwoch, pr. o-ruwoch-an, -ei, p.p. ko-ruwoch-an.
- **Adviser**, ki-ruwok-in, pl. ki-ruwok-u.
- **Afraid, to be**: r. wagh, pr. o-wegh-an, -ei, p.p. ka-wagh-an.
- **After**(*wards*), suffix -yăʻ; o-wet-an(-ei)-yăʻ, *I will come afterwards*.
  (*behind*), lĕt.
- **Afterbirth**, par-ua, pl. par-oi, poron-oi.

*(Signature at foot: H 2)*

*(Page 100)*

- **Afternoon**, perghiet-kwin.
- **Again**: *again and again*, ñgat: ñgat-ñgat.
- **Age** (*vide* **Year**), keny, pl. keny-is *or* kainy, pl. kainy-is.
  *An age*, pĕn.
- **Agitate, to**: r. timtim, pr. o-timtim-an, -ei, p.p. ko-timtim-an.
  *To shake*: r. sach, pr. o-sech-an, -ei, p.p. ka-sach-an.
- **Ago**, keny-isioʻ, kainy-isioʻ.
  *Long ago*, ken'-man, kain'-man.
  *How long ago*, kenyis-ata, kainy-is-ata.
  *Five days ago*, kainy-isioʻ, ōsis mūt.
- **Agriculturist**, chi-pa-pagh, pl. pi-pa-pagh.
- **Aim, to** (*either of attempt or of aiming a spear*): r. rŏs, pr. o-ros-an, -ei, p.p. kô-ros-an. *Try to go*, ros-a wiy-a.
- **Air**, yamat.
- **Alike**, lata-nyô-nyi, le-nyô-nyi; Pi'chuno lata-nyô-nyi, *those men are alike*.
- **Alive, to be**: r. ril, pr. o-ril-an, -ei, p.p. ko-ril-an.
- **All**, tukul, tokol, lopai. *Vide* p. 74.
  *All together*, tukul-okoñgô.
  *All five*, tokol-kô-mūt.
- **Allow, to**: Turn by 'give' with pron. followed by subj., kun' o-wet-ei, *give me leave to go*; kun'-anan-a-gh, *let me work*.
- **Alone** (*by oneself*), Ande-ungit, &c. *Vide* p. 73.
- **Aloud**, têrgha.
- **Already**, express by pres. perf. tense.
- **Also**, ñgat.
- **Alter, to** (*act.*): r. warach, pr. o-warech-an, -ei, p.p. ka-warach-an; (ii) r. wal. *Vide* **Change**.
- **Although** [use the conditional mood].
- **Amazed, to be**: r. iyēr, pr. o-iyer-an, -ei, p.p. ka-iyer-an.
- **Amend, to**: r. gh, pr. o-gh-an, -ei, p.p. ka-gh-an.
- **Amidst**, kwin; lalua kwin, *In the midst of the river*.
- **Amount to, to**: r. t, pr. o-t-an, p.p. ka-t-an.
- **Amulet**, setan, pl. *id.* (Arab ?).
  (*medicine*), saket (coll.).
- **Amuse, to**: r. piren, pr. o-piren-j-in-an, -ei, p.p. ko-piren-j-an.
- **Amusement**, piren-ei, pl. piron-sut.
- **Ancestor** (*male*), kukŏʻ, pl. kuko-tin.
  (*female*), kŏkō, pl. koko-tin.
- **Ancient**, pa-kuko: pa-keny-isioʻ, pa-keny, pa-kainy.
- **And**, ngô.
- **Angry, to be**: r. narech, pr. o-narech-an, -ei, p.p. ka-narech-an.
- **Animal**, tiuny, pl. tiung-en.
- **Ankle(-bone)**, kôwô-monion, pl. kô-monion, koiyo-monion.
- **Anklet** (*of bells*), *for male or female*, kurugur, pl. kurugur-is.
- **Annoy, to**: r. rum, pr. o-rum-an, p.p. ka-rum-an; (ii) r. ghom.
- **Another**, anga, pl. w-alak.
- **Answer, to**: r. siropant, pr. o-siropent-oi-an, p.p. ko-siropant-an. Ka-siropant-oʻ, *He has answered*.
- **Ant-bear** (*ardvaak*), kalambas, pl. kalambos.
- **Ant-hill**, tul, pl. tul-un-oi.
- **Ants**:
  *In general, and in particular black soldier ants*, sungok (coll.).
  *White*, mengarich, pl. mengarich-ai; (ii) taugh (coll.).
  *Termite (used for food)*, piyapai-wa, pl. piyapai.
  *Brown*, pirĕch (coll.).
  *Black (having an offensive smell)*, kamukōk (coll.).

*(Page 101)*

- **Antelope**:
  *Impala*, chemel, pl. chemel-tin.
  *Grantii, &c.*, akuete, pl. akuete-tin.
  *Eland*, wabet, pl. wabet-in; (ii) kip-tuko, pl. kip-tuko-tin.
  *Oryx*, adar, pl. adar-tin.
  *Dik-dik*, sīran, pl. siron.
  *Kudu (greater)*, sarômat, pl. saromit.
  *Waterbuck*, amusomus, pl. amusomus-tin.
  (*lit. 'the angry one'*), chem-nerechei, pl. chem-nerechei-tin.¹
  *Hartebeeste*, chem-nyokos-oʻ, pl. chem-nyokos-tin.
  *Topi*, asarich, pl. asari'-tin.
  *Bush-buck*, pogh-an, pl. pogh-in.
  *Reed-buck*, parañg, pl. parañg-tin.
- **Anus**, maso-wa, pl. maso-ei.
- **Anybody**, chi-tokol.
- **Anything**, kī, kue: ipa ke-cham bat, *Take what you will*.
- **Anywhere**, ketukōn.
- **Apart** [*far, id.*], lōh, pl. loh-w-ech.
- **Appear, to**: use pass. of 'see': ko-sow-iy-anan, *I appear*.
- **Appearance** (*forehead*), tokach, pl. tokuch.
- **Approach, to**: o-wet-an otô legit-ô chemel, *I will approach the impala*.
- **Approach!** imp. nyun-ô! pl. a-pkan-a!
- **Approve, to**: r. cham [*vide* **accept**, **love**, &c.].
- **Argue, to**: r. ti, pr. o-ti-soi-an, p.p. kô-ti-i-an.
- **Argument**, tisot.
- **Arise, to**: r. ñget, pr. o-ñget-an, -ei, p.p. ka-ñget-an.
- **Arm**, hegh, pl. hegh-w-un [*gh almost surd*].
  *Forearm*, asibangaru, pl. asibangaru-tin.
  *Upper arm*, charŭt, pl. cherūt.
  *Arm-ornament of ivory*, chapos, pl. chapos-ai.
  *Arm-pit*, kolkol, pl. kulkul-w-ak.
- **Arrange, to**: r. gh, pr. o-gh-an, p.p. ka-gh-an.
- **Arrive at, to**: r. tīt, pr. o-tīt-oi-an, p.p. ka-tīt-an.
- **Arrow**, kôtat, pl. kôti.
  *Feathers*, karor (pl. of karor-ion *used collectively*).
  *Shaft*, ket, pl. *id.*
  *Barb (iron harpoon shape)*, pem, pl. pem-w-on.
  *(iron square)*, olkich-oʻ, pl. ol-kich-ei.
  *(plain rounded iron)*, rokos, pl. rokos-ai.
  *Wooden head*, supĕt, pl. supīt.
  *Poison*, mwônô (coll.).
- **Arrow** *for bleeding cattle, sheep, &c.*, terem-a, pl. terem-w-oi.
- **Artery**, tĕkĕt, pl. tīkīt.
- **Article, an**, ki, kue, pl. tukun.
- **As**, lata: nya-lat'-i-chem-enyi, *as you please*.
- **Ascend, to**: r. lany, pr. o-lony-an, p.p. ka-lany-an.
- **Ash**, hŏr, pl. hŏr-en.
- **Ashamed, to be**: r. tech, pr. o-tech-an, p.p. ka-tech-an.
- **Aside, to put**: r. ror, pr. o-ror-an, -ei, p.p. kô-ror-an.
  *To go aside*: r. rōp, pr. o-rop-an, -ei, p.p. ka-rop-an.
- **Ask, to**: r. tērp, pr. o-tērp-an, -ei, p.p. ka-tērp-an.
  *To ask for*, r. som, pr. o-som-an, -ei, p.p. ka-som-an.
- **Ass**, sigir-ioʻ, pl. sigir-ai.
- **Assemblage**, *place of*, kōkwa, pl. kōkwon; kokwo-ten.
- **Assemble, to**: (*intr.*) r. nyur, pr. o-nyur-un-an, p.p. kô-nyur-u-an.
- **Assembly**, ka-nyur-ioʻ.

> ¹ Root *nerech*, angry, 'Because it has such an angry face.'

*(Page 102)*

- **Assent, to**: r. cham [*vide* **accept**, &c.].
- **Assist, to**: r. rem, pr. o-rem-jin(chin)-an, p.p. kô-rem-j(ch)-an.
- **Astonished, to be**: r. iyer [*vide* **amazed**]; (ii) r. non, o-nōn-an, p.p. ka-nōn-an.
- **At.** [By position] mit-o lalua, *he is at the river*. ôto (?).
  *At first*, nya-pa-tagh.
  *At home, to be*, r. mit, pr. o-mit-an, -ei, p.p. ko-mit-an.
  *At once*, lawel.
  *At the top*, yim.
  *At the bottom*, mwin.
- **Attack, to**: r. por, pr. o-por-soi-an, p.p. kô-por-i-an.
- **Attempt, to** [*vide* **Aim**, r. ros].
- **Attend, to** (*wait*): r. kany, pr. o-keny-an, -ei, p.p. ka-kany-an.
  *Wait for a person*: r. med, pr. o-med-oi-an, p.p. ka-med-an.
- **Aunt** (*maternal*), iyu-nyan anga, *my other mother*.
  (*paternal*), cheb-kŏʻ, pl. cheb-ko-tin.
- **Avoid, to**: r. rŭp, pr. o-rup-an, -ei, p.p. ko-rup-an.
  *To get out of the way*: r. nāch, pr. o-nôch-an, p.p. ka-nāch-an.
  *Get out of the way!* mal-a kegh!
- **Await, to**: r. kany [*vide* **to attend**]; (ii) r. med [*vide* **attend**].
- **Awaken, to**: r. ñget, pr. o-ñget-an, p.p. ka-ñget-an.
- **Awaken, to** (*act.*): *id.* with objective suffix, o-ñget-inyi, *I will wake you*.
- **Away, to go**: r. wet, pr. o-wet-an, -ei, p.p. ka-wet-an; r. w, pr. o-w-an, p.p. ko-w-an.
  *He is away*, ma-mi-nye.
- **Axe**, öiw-oʻ, pl. öiw-ei.
  *Small bill-hook*, was-aʻ, pl. was-ai.

### B

- **Baboon**, maiyos, pl. maiyos-oi.
- **Baby** (*of either sex*), mŭnuñg, pl. mūnuñg; *my baby*, monde-nyan, pl. mon-echan.
- **Back, to go**: r. mel, pr. o-mel-an, p.p. ka-mel-an.
- **Back** (*of a human being or animal*), korôt, pl. korôt-ai.
  (*at base of spine*), sukulim, pl. *id.*
- **Back-bone**, patăgh, pl. patōgh.
- **Bad** (*adj.*), ghaʻ, pl. gha-ch (*sometimes pron.* thāʻ, tha-ch).
  *A bad fellow*, torōno, pl. torôno-tin.
- **Bad, to go**, r. nun. 3rd sing. pr. nun-ei; 3rd sing. p.p. ka-nun.
- **Bad-tempered**, korōm, pl. korōm-ech.
- **Bag, a**, melŏt, pl. melūt.
  *Large bag or sack*, sambur, pl. sambur-us.
- **Bake, to**: r. pal, pr. o-pĕl-an, -ei, p.p. ka-păl-an.
- **Bald** (*adj.*), paiyos, pl. paiyos-ech.
- **Bale, to** (*to dig for water*): r. was, pr. o-wes-an, ei, p.p. ka-was-an.
- **Bamboo**, teichan, pl. tērka.
- **Banana**, *wild*, sasur-wa, pl. sasôr.
- **Banded** (*i.e. striped*), kakekir, pl. kekekir.
- **Banish, to**: r. wirt, pr. o-wirt-oi-an, pp. ka-wirt-an.
- **Bank** (*of a river*), endakwagh, pl. endakwai'-tin.
  *To cross to the other bank*: r. ogh, pr. ō-gh-soi-an, p.p. kō-gh-i-an.
- **Barber, a**, ruŏk-in, pl. ruok-ŭ.
- **Bare** (*adj.*), cherut, pl. cherut-ech.
- **Bark** (*of a tree*), pērt-aʻ, pl. pērt-oi.
- **Bark, to** (*of a dog*): r. pkwonsi, pr. 3rd sing. pkwonsī, p.p. 3rd sing. ke-pkwonsi-oʻ, *irreg.*

*(Page 103)*

- **Barrel** (*placed in trees to attract bees*), mweghin, pl. mweghin-t-is.
- **Barren person, a** (*s.*), sôno, pl. sônon; kôrkôʻ nya le-sôno, *a barren woman* (also used of animals).
- **Basin, a**, otup-aʻ, pl. otup-in; otup-ai.
- **Basket, a** (*of any kind*), kerep, pl. kerep-ai.
- **Bat, a**, rēres, pl. reres-ai.
- **Bathe, to**: r. mwat, pr. o-mwet-an, -ei, p.p. ka-mwat-an.
- **Battle, a**, luk, pl. luk-us.
- **Be, to**: r. i, pr. 3rd sing. mi; *vide* p. 86, *irreg.*
  *To be present*: r. mit, pr. o-mit-an, -ei, p.p. ko-mit-an.
  *To stay*, r. tĕp, pr. o-tep-oi-an, p.p. kô-tep-an.
- **Bead** (*in general*), sanai-yan, pl. sanai.
  *Of ostrich egg-shell*, sem-nyan, pl. săm.
  *Small black*, nonongo-ion, pl. nonongo.
  *Cowrie shells*, seker-ion, pl. sekēr.
  *Pierced iron wire*, ter-ua, pl. ter-oi.
- **Beak** (*of a bird*), kote-tarit, pl. koto-wa-tarit.
- **Bear, to** (*children*): r. iyiy, pr. o-iyiy-an, -ei, p.p. ka-iyiy-an.
  *Pregnant* (*adj.*), tesiar, pl. tesiēr-ei.
  *To carry on back*: r. kasan, pr. o-kesen-an, -ei, p.p. ka-kasan-an.
  *To carry*, r. p, pr. o-p-an, -ei, p.p. ko-p-an, imp. ipa, pl. opa.
- **Beard**, tamogh, pl. tamo'-w-ai.
- **Beast**, tiuny, pl. tiung-en.
  *Of prey*, kuam-chi, pl. kuam-pich.
- **Beat, to**: r. păr, pr. o-per-an, -ei, p.p. ka-par-an.
  *With a stick*, r. muny, pr. o-muny-an, -ei, p.p. kō-muny-an.
  *To conquer*: r. pēl, pr. o-pel-an, p.p. kô-pel-an.
- **Beautiful**, karam, pl. karam-ech.
- **Beauty**, konyen (the pl. of koñg, 'an eye').
- **Because**, omba, mba, mbe; omba ilat, *because of the rain*.
  *Why?*, mbe-ne; mbene se¹-kabkaʻ, *why have they come?*
- **Beckon, to**: kura hegh chi chino, *call that man with your hand*.
- **Become great, to**: r. gh, pr. o-gh-ietun-an, pp. ko-gh-ietu-an.
- **Bed**, pareiy, pl. pareiy-us.
  *Head of bed*, met-o.
  *Foot of bed*, kel-ien.
- **Bees** (*generally*), sakam (coll.).
  *Earth bees*, kasem-nyon, pl. kasem-is.
  *Drone*, kakopur, pl. kakopur-tin.
  *Bees' nest*, pōnat, pl. pōnīt.
  *Beeswax*, temen-ioʻ, pl. temen.
  *To take a nest*: r. lainy, pr. o-loiny-an, -ei, p.p. ka-lainy-an.
- **Beetle**, torwok, pl. torwok-ai.
  *A kind of (?)*, tamu, pl. tamu-tin.
  *Another kind of (?)*, metarañg, pl. metarañg-ai.
- **Before**, (*of time*) tôma; (*of place*) tagh; *In front of the house*, tagh-pa-kô.
  *Before he heard*, tôma-nye ke-lim.
  *To go before*: r. tôwôgh, pr. o-tōwōgh-an, p.p. kô-tôwôgh-an.
- **Beg, to**: r. sōm, pr. o-som-an, -ei, p.p. ka-som-an.
- **Beget, to**: r. iyiy; *vide* **bear**.
- **Beggar, a**, sōm-in, pl. som-u.

- **Begin, to**: r. nyai, pr. o-nyoi-an, -ei, p.p. ka-nyai-an.
- **Behind** (*adv.*), let.
  (*prep.*) let-u.
- **Belch, to**: r. siegh, pr. o-siegh-an, -ei, p.p. ka-siegh-an.
- **Believe, to**, r. ñgerak, pr. o-ñgerok-an, p.p. ka-ñgerak-an.
- **Bell** (*for man's arm*), kirugur, pl. kirugur-is.
  (*for oxen*), kadoñgut, pl. kadoñgut-in.

  *Near*, legĕt.

  *Hawk*, sirer-a, pl. sirer-tin.
  *Humming-bird*, chesaitek, pl. chesaitek-tin.
  *Owl*, yukuk, pl. yukuk-ei, yukuk-tin.

  *Swallow*, wesian, pl. wes-oi.
  *Vulture*, matŏny, pl. matōñg.
  *Woodpecker*, blaiket, pl. blaiket-is.
- **Bite, to**: r. ser, pr. o-ser-an, -ei, pp. ka-ser-an.
- **Bitter** (*adj.*), ñgwan, pl. ñgwan-ech.
- **Black** (*adj.*), tōgh, pl. tu-w-ech.
- **Blacksmith**, ki-toñg-in, pl. ki-toñg-ĕ.
- **Bladder**, tepil, pl. tepil-tin.
- **Blade of a spear**, muko'-ngôt, pl. mukon-in-ngôt-wa.

- **Bleat, to** (*of sheep and goats*): r. ghit, pr. o-ghut-ei (irreg.), p.p. ko-ghit-an.
- **Bleed, to**: r. char, pr. o-cher-an, -ei, p.p. ka-char-an.
- **Blind** (*adj.*), korôt-ei, pl. korôt-en-i.
  *A blind person*, cheb-korôt; ocha korôt-ei, *I am blind*.

- **Blister, a**, katoltolet, pl. *id.*
- **Blood**, kīsen (coll.).
- **Blow, to** (*of wind, a horn, &c.*): r. kut, pr. o-kut-an, -ei, p.p. ka-kūt-an.
  *To blow the nose*: r. rum, pr. o-rum-an, -ei, p.p. ko-rum-an.

- **Boat**, kadich, pl. kadi'-tin (En-jemusi).

- **Boil** (*trans.*). *Vide under* **Cook**.
- **Boil, to** (*trans.*): r. kôt, pr. o-kôt-an, ko-kôt-an.
  (*intrans.*): r. ka-kôtoʻ pogh, *the water has boiled*.
- **Boils**, makaskas (coll.).

- **Bow, to**: *vide* **to bend**; r. ñge-ruk.

- **Brass-wire**, tai, pl. tau-w-oi.

  *To pound*: r. lu, pr. o-lu-an, -ei, pp. ko-lu-an; (ii) r. nagh.
  *To tear*: r. karer, pr. o-kerer-an, -ei, pp. ka-karer-an.
  *To break through*: r. wir, pr. o-wir-an, -ei, p.p. ka-wir-an.
  *To break wind*: r. juz, pr. o-juz-an, -ei, pp. ko-juz-an.

- **Breasts** (*of men and animals*), ketan (coll.). s. *id.*
- **Breath** (*s.*), ki-muny.

- **Brew wine, to**: r. riach, pr. o-riech-an, -ei, pp. ka-riach-an.

- **Bring, to**: r. p, pr. o-p-an, pp. ko-p-an.

- **Brother**, weri, pl. werkoʻ.
  *My, your, his brother, &c.*, weri-nja, pl. werkecha.
- **Brother-in-law**; *wife's brother or sister's husband*, ŏra, pl. ŏra-tin.
  *Husband's brother*, aiwan, pl. aiwan-tin.

- **Bullock**, egh, pl. egh-in.
- **Bulrush**, kamatigh-an; coll. and pl. kamatigh.
- **Burn, to** (*trans.*): r. nagh, pr. o-negh-an, -ei, p.p. ka-nagh-an; *or by* r. ten, o-ten-an, -ei, maʻ, *I put fire to*.
  (*intrans.*): r. lach, *vide infra*.
  *To be consumed*: r. lach, pr. o-lach-an, pp. ka-lach-an.
  *To roast*: r. pal, pr. o-pel-an, -ei, pp. ko-pal-an.
  *To kindle*: r. ten, o-ten-a' maʻ.
  *To pile up (the fire)*: r. iyin, pr. o-iyin-an, -ei, p.p. ka-iyin-an.

  (*intrans.*): r. igh, pr. o-igh-an, -ei.

- **Bustle, to**: r. kas, pr. o-kos-kos-an, -ei, p.p. ka-kaskas-an.

- **Butter**, mwai'-pa-cheʻ, lit. *fat of milk*.
